## Get started with PayPal REST APIs
<https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started>

- [snippet] 2. Get an access token: curl -v -X POST "https://api-m.sandbox.paypal.com/v1/oauth2/token" \ — `curl -v -X POST "https://api-m.sandbox.paypal.com/v1/oauth2/token" \` — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started#2-get-an-access-token
- [snippet] Sample response: { — `{` — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started#sample-response
- [definition] Get started with PayPal REST APIs — Current PayPal APIs use REST, authenticate with OAuth 2.0 access tokens, and return HTTP response codes and JSON responses. You can test US integrations with a <a href="https://developer.paypal.com/home/">PayPal Developer</a>  account. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started#get-started-with-paypal-rest-apis
- [definition] 1. Get your client ID and client secret — PayPal integrations use a client ID and client secret to authenticate API calls: — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started#1-get-your-client-id-and-client-secret
- [definition] 2. Get an access token — You exchange your client ID and client secret for an access token, which you use for authentication when calling PayPal REST APIs. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started#2-get-an-access-token
- [definition] Sample response — PayPal returns an access token and the number of seconds for which the access token is valid, as shown in the following example. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started#sample-response
- [definition] Make API calls — When you make API calls, replace `ACCESS-TOKEN` with your access token in the authorization header: `-H Authorization: Bearer ACCESS-TOKEN`. When your access token expires, call `/v1/oauth2/token` again to request a new access token. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started#make-api-calls
- [definition] 3. Get sandbox account credentials — The PayPal sandbox is a test environment that mirrors real-world transactions. By default, PayPal developer accounts have 2 sandbox accounts: a personal account for buying and a business account for selling. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started#3-get-sandbox-account-credentials
- [concept] Current PayPal APIs use REST, authenticate with OAuth 2.0 access tokens, and return HTTP response codes and JSON responses. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started
- [fact] You need a PayPal Business account to go live with integrations or test integrations outside of the US. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started
- [concept] A client ID identifies an app and is required to get a PayPal payment button and standard credit and debit card fields. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started
- [fact] To call PayPal APIs, you exchange your client ID and client secret for an access token. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started
- [actionable] Select Apps & Credentials in the Dashboard to create a new project or use the default application in the REST API apps section. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started
- [fact] New PayPal accounts come with a default application in the REST API apps section. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started
- [actionable] To get an access token using cURL, call the PayPal OAuth API endpoint provided in the documentation. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started
- [actionable] In Postman, set the verb to POST and enter https://api-m.sandbox.paypal.com/v1/oauth2/token as the request URL. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started
- [actionable] In Postman, set Authorization type to Basic Auth, enter your client ID as Username and client secret as Password. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started
- [actionable] In the Postman Body tab, select x-www-form-urlencoded, set KEY to grant_type, and VALUE to client_credentials. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started
- [fact] PayPal returns an access token and the number of seconds for which the access token is valid in the response. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started
- [actionable] When making API calls, replace ACCESS-TOKEN with your access token in the authorization header: -H Authorization: Bearer ACCESS-TOKEN. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started
- [actionable] When your access token expires, call /v1/oauth2/token again to request a new access token. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started
- [concept] The PayPal sandbox is a test environment that mirrors real-world transactions and allows you to watch money move between accounts. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started
- [fact] By default, PayPal developer accounts have two sandbox accounts: a personal account for buying and a business account for selling. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started
- [actionable] To get sandbox credentials, select Testing Tools > Sandbox Accounts in the Developer Dashboard. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started
- [actionable] Select View/Edit Account to see mock information such as the account email address and a system-generated password. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started
- [actionable] Sign in with personal sandbox credentials at sandbox.paypal.com/signin/ in one browser and business credentials in a separate browser. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/get-started

- [definition] Handling API responses when integrating with PayPal APIs — Correctly processing API responses ensures that your application can gracefully manage successful payments, declines, errors, and edge cases. This improves user experience, reduces failed transactions, and helps maintain compliance with PCI DSS, the security standards for handling credit card data. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses#handling-api-responses-when-integrating-with-paypal-apis
- [definition] Response handling flow — This image shows the basic flow for handling API responses: — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses#response-handling-flow
- [definition] Understanding response types — PayPal API responses generally fall into two categories: — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses#understanding-response-types
- [definition] Successful responses — A successful response has a status code between 200 and 299 and includes the requested data: — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses#successful-responses
- [definition] Error responses — Error responses have status codes outside the 200-299 range and include error details: — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses#error-responses
- [definition] HTTP status codes and error handling — The following table shows common HTTP status codes from PayPal APIs and what they mean: — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses#http-status-codes-and-error-handling
- [definition] How REST API responses work — PayPal APIs send back structured JSON answers when you make requests about payments, orders, and accounts. These answers include status codes, results, and error details. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses#how-rest-api-responses-work
- [definition] Error handling — PayPal gives you specific error codes and messages to help you fix issues. By checking these codes and showing helpful messages to users, your app can guide people through problems and make payments easier. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses#error-handling
- [definition] Common PayPal API error codes — Here are the most common error codes you'll see and how to fix them: — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses#common-paypal-api-error-codes
- [definition] Testing your error handling — PayPal offers tools to test how your app handles errors. These tools let you create test errors and declined payments without real problems. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses#testing-your-error-handling
- [definition] Handling card declines — When a card is declined or payment fails, your app needs to handle it smoothly. Your system should spot these issues and give users clear feedback and options to try again. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses#handling-card-declines
- [definition] Integration — This section explains how to handle PayPal API responses and errors in both back-end and front-end environments. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses#integration
- [definition] Back-end — The back end handles secure tasks like creating payments, checking transactions, and storing API keys. It talks directly to PayPal's APIs, processes responses, handles errors, and keeps sensitive data safe. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses#back-end
- [definition] Front-end — The front end interacts with users, starts payments, and shows feedback based on API responses. Good response handling ensures users know the payment status, can retry if needed, and get clear guidance when something goes wrong. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses#front-end
- [definition] Security testing tips — Testing your security setup is vital to protect against threats. Consider these key tests: — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses#security-testing-tips
- [definition] Testing your response handling — Follow these steps to test how your app handles PayPal responses: — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses#testing-your-response-handling
- [concept] PayPal API responses are structured JSON answers containing status codes, results, and error details for payments, orders, and accounts. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses
- [fact] Successful PayPal API responses have a status code between 200 and 299 and include the requested data. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses
- [fact] Error responses from PayPal APIs have status codes outside the 200-299 range and include specific error details. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses
- [actionable] Check the HTTP status code and response body for every API call to ensure secure integration. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses
- [problem] Exposing sensitive error details to users is a security pitfall that must be avoided during error handling. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses
- [idea] Use PayPal's sandbox tools to simulate errors and test how your application handles them before going live. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses
- [actionable] Create specific test errors by adding the header `PayPal-Mock-Response: {"mock_application_codes":"INSTRUMENT_DECLINED"}` to your test API requests. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses
- [problem] Front-end code must never send API keys or tokens directly, as this compromises security. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses
- [concept] Back-end environments handle secure tasks like creating payments, checking transactions, and storing API keys while talking directly to PayPal's APIs. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses
- [idea] For mobile apps, use native SDKs when possible to achieve a better user experience and handle spotty connections. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses
- [fact] Payflow transaction responses are documented separately from REST API responses in the Payflow integration guide. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses
- [actionable] Test with expired tokens to verify that your token refresh system functions correctly during auth testing. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses
- [problem] Sensitive data should not be saved in logs, and error messages must not reveal sensitive details to users. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses
- [idea] Plan for offline scenarios in mobile apps where the connection is lost during payment processing. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses
- [actionable] Create test merchant and buyer accounts to set up a testing environment for response handling verification. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/handling-api-responses

- [parameter] `count`: Type=integer; Description=The number of items to list in the response. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests#query-parameters
- [parameter] `end_time`: Type=integer; Description=The end date and time for the range to show in the response, in <a href="https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc3339#section-5.6">Internet date and time format</a>. For example, `end_time=2025-03-07T11:00:00Z`. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests#query-parameters
- [parameter] `page`: Type=integer; Description=The page number indicating which set of items will be returned in the response. For example, the combination of `page=1` and `page_size=20` returns the first 20 items. The combination of `page=2` and `page_size=20` returns items 21 through 40. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests#query-parameters
- [parameter] `page_size`: Type=integer; Description=The number of items to return in the response. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests#query-parameters
- [parameter] `total_count_required`: Type=boolean; Description=Indicates whether to show the total count in the response. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests#query-parameters
- [parameter] `sort_by`: Type=string; Description=Sorts the payments in the response by a specified value, such as the create time or update time. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests#query-parameters
- [parameter] `sort_order`: Type=string; Description=Sorts the items in the response in ascending or descending order. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests#query-parameters
- [parameter] `start_id`: Type=string; Description=The ID of the starting resource in the response. When results are paged, you can use the `next_id` value as the `start_id` to continue with the next set of results. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests#query-parameters
- [parameter] `start_index`: Type=integer; Description=The `start_index` parameter lets you specify the position in the list where results should begin. For example, setting `start_index=2` skips the first item and shows results from the second item onward. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests#query-parameters
- [parameter] `start_time`: Type=string; Description=The start date and time for the range to show in the response, in Internet date and time format. For example, `start_time=2025-03-06T11:00:00Z`. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests#query-parameters

- [definition] Query parameters — > **Note:** Not all pagination parameters are available for all APIs. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests#query-parameters
- [definition] Example request: create invoice — PayPal's REST APIs support standard HTTP methods - `GET`, `POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH`, `DELETE`. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests#example-request-create-invoice
- [concept] PayPal REST APIs authenticate using OAuth 2.0 access tokens and return HTTP response codes with JSON-encoded responses. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests
- [fact] Sandbox requests target https://api-m.sandbox.paypal.com, while Live production requests target https://api-m.paypal.com. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests
- [concept] First-party calls are made on behalf of your own business, also known as direct merchant integration. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests
- [concept] Third-party calls are made on behalf of another merchant, common for marketplaces and platforms, also called multiparty or partner integration. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests
- [fact] The PayPal-Auth-Assertion header is optional and required only for platforms and marketplaces handling payments for multiple merchants. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests
- [concept] The PayPal-Auth-Assertion header is a JSON Web Token (JWT) that identifies the specific merchant for each API call. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests
- [actionable] Build an unsigned JWT by setting the algorithm to 'alg': 'none' in the header and using an empty string for the signature. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests
- [fact] The PayPal-Auth-Assertion payload must include the platform's client_id as 'iss' and the merchant's PayPal payer ID as 'payer_id'. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests
- [fact] The PayPal-Partner-Attribution-Id header is required for platforms and marketplaces to identify the platform and track transactions. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests
- [actionable] Find your Build Notation (BN) code by selecting Apps & Credentials, choosing the app, and viewing the Reports section under App Settings. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests
- [concept] The PayPal-Mock-Response header allows simulating errors by passing an error code through the mock_application_codes parameter. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests
- [fact] The PayPal-Request-Id header is recommended for all POST and PUT calls to enforce idempotency and prevent duplicate transactions. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests
- [fact] PayPal stores the PayPal-Request-Id for up to 45 days, allowing retries without duplicating actions during this period. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests
- [problem] A 401 Unauthorized error indicates that platform credentials are invalid or you lack permission to act on behalf of the merchant. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests
- [problem] A 400 Bad Request error suggests the JWT format is incorrect or required fields are missing. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests
- [problem] A 403 Forbidden error occurs if the merchant has not granted your platform appropriate permissions. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests
- [actionable] Append query parameters to the endpoint URL using a question mark for the first parameter and ampersands to separate subsequent ones. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests
- [fact] The page_size=4 parameter limits the response to 4 items per page in pagination requests. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests
- [fact] Setting total_count_required=true instructs PayPal to include the total count of items in the API response. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests
- [idea] Always URL-encode parameter names and values if they contain special characters before appending them to the endpoint URL. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/make-api-requests

## Rate limiting with PayPal REST APIs
<https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/rate-limiting>

- [snippet] What to do if you hit a rate limit: function apiRequest(endpoint, data) { — `// Example error handling for rate limits` — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/rate-limiting#what-to-do-if-you-hit-a-rate-limit
- [definition] Rate limiting with PayPal REST APIs — Rate limiting restricts the number of API requests you can make in a specific time frame. When you exceed this limit, PayPal's servers may deny further requests until your usage drops below the limit. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/rate-limiting#rate-limiting-with-paypal-rest-apis
- [definition] How to prepare for rate limiting — Here's what you can do to reduce the chances of hitting a rate limit: — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/rate-limiting#how-to-prepare-for-rate-limiting
- [definition] What to do if you hit a rate limit — If your API requests start failing due to rate limiting, follow these steps: — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/rate-limiting#what-to-do-if-you-hit-a-rate-limit
- [concept] Rate limiting restricts the number of API requests you can make in a specific time frame to protect the system from overload. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/rate-limiting
- [fact] When you exceed the rate limit, PayPal's servers may deny further requests until your usage drops below the limit. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/rate-limiting
- [fact] Receiving a `429` status code indicates that too many requests were sent and might signal anomalous traffic. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/rate-limiting
- [problem] PayPal does not publish exact rate limits because they vary depending on the API, environment, and circumstances. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/rate-limiting
- [problem] Excessive polling is a common reason PayPal enforces rate limits instead of using webhooks. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/rate-limiting
- [problem] Token misuse occurs when developers fail to reuse OAuth 2.0 tokens and repeatedly fetch new ones. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/rate-limiting
- [idea] Use webhooks instead of polling to let PayPal send updates directly to your system without constant requests. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/rate-limiting
- [idea] Cache OAuth 2.0 tokens by securely storing and reusing them until they expire rather than creating new ones for every request. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/rate-limiting
- [idea] Optimize your requests by minimizing unnecessary calls, avoiding frequent data requests, and combining requests whenever possible. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/rate-limiting
- [actionable] Check your API logs to diagnose why a rate limit was triggered and look for patterns in request frequency or behavior. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/rate-limiting
- [actionable] Reduce request frequency by slowing down rates or spreading requests out over time to stay within limits. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/rate-limiting
- [actionable] Implement retry with exponential backoff by increasing the delay between requests each time a request fails. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/rate-limiting
- [idea] In a production environment, use a more robust storage mechanism and implement proper secure token management. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/rate-limiting
- [idea] For production systems, consider using a production-grade logging system and more sophisticated retry strategies with maximum retry limits. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/rate-limiting
- [problem] Suspicious patterns of behavior may be flagged by PayPal as atypical or potentially harmful, triggering rate limits. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/rate-limiting
- [fact] PayPal uses rate limits to ensure system stability when traffic spikes occur due to user activity or system events. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/rate-limiting
- [question] How should developers implement error handling for different types of API responses in a production environment? — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/rate-limiting

- [parameter] `AMOUNT_MISMATCH`: Ensure the total amount equals the sum of line items, taxes, and discounts. The API returns this error if the sale amounts are not reflected in the request. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `CARD_EXPIRED`: Show the error to the payer and ask them to use a different card. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `CANNOT_BE_NEGATIVE`: Enter a positive amount with no more than two decimal places. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `CANNOT_BE_ZERO_OR_NEGATIVE`: Enter a positive, non-zero amount with no more than two decimal places. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `CURRENCY_NOT_SUPPORTED`: Use a PayPal-supported currency. Make sure the receiving PayPal account accepts the currency. For a list of supported currencies, see [Currency codes](https://developer.paypal.com/docs/api/rest/reference/currency-codes/currency-codes/). — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `DECIMAL_PRECISION`: Round the amount to 2 decimal places and try again. If the issue persists, contact [PayPal support](https://www.paypal.com/us/cshelp/technical) and provide the `debug_id` from the API response. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `DECIMALS_NOT_SUPPORTED`: Adjust the amount to match the number of decimal places the currency supports. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `DUPLICATE_INVOICE_ID`: Use a different `invoice_id`. If you must reuse the same `invoice_id` and the issue persists, contact [PayPal support](https://www.paypal.com/us/cshelp/technical). — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `INCOMPATIBLE_PARAMETER_VALUE`: Make sure the parameters in the API request match the expected data types. For more information, see [Authentication](https://developer.paypal.com/api/rest/authentication/). — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `INVALID_PARAMETER_SYNTAX`: Make sure the API request JSON is correct and follows the PayPal API request format. If the issue persists, contact [PayPal support](https://www.paypal.com/us/cshelp/technical) and provide the `debug_id` from the API response. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `INVALID_PARAMETER_VALUE`: Enter a valid parameter value. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `INVALID_RESOURCE_ID`: Check the resource ID and try again. If the resource ID belongs to a different PayPal account, check the scopes and permissions for the receiving account. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `INVALID_STRING_LENGTH`: Make sure text fields are not too long and include all required data. For string length requirements, see the [Orders API](https://developer.paypal.com/docs/api/orders/v2/#orders_create). — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `ITEM_TOTAL_MISMATCH`: Make sure the item totals match the quantity total. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `MAX_NUMBER_OF_PAYMENT_ATTEMPTS_EXCEEDED`: Ask the payer to use a different payment method. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `MISSING_REQUIRED_PARAMETER`: Make sure the JSON follows the PayPal API request format and includes all [required parameters](https://developer.paypal.com/docs/api/orders/v2/#orders_create). If the issue persists, contact [PayPal support](https://www.paypal.com/us/cshelp/technical) and provide the `debug_id` from the API response. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `ORDER_ALREADY_AUTHORIZED`: To proceed, [call capture](https://developer.paypal.com/docs/api/orders/v2/#orders_capture) because the funds are authorized. If the authorization is more than 3 days old, reauthorize the funds before capturing. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `ORDER_ALREADY_CAPTURED`: No action needed. Use a `GET` call on the order ID to get the capture ID or PayPal transaction ID. For multi-capture or split shipments, use `intent=AUTHORIZE`. See [Capture authorized payment](https://developer.paypal.com/docs/api/payments/v2/#authorizations_capture) for more information. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `ORDER_NOT_APPROVED`: Ask the payer to complete PayPal checkout again to approve the order. Redirect them to the `'rel':'approve'` URL in the HATEOAS links from the create order call or provide a valid `payment_source` in the request. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `PAYEE_ACCOUNT_RESTRICTED`: Contact PayPal customer support to lift restrictions on the receiving account. If you are a marketplace, ask the seller to resolve restrictions with PayPal. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `PAYEE_NOT_CONSENTED`: Make sure the API caller has consent to collect partner fees for the payee. Add `PARTNER_FEE` to the capabilities during [signup](https://developer.paypal.com/docs/multiparty/seller-onboarding/before-payment/#generate-a-signup-link). If `PARTNER_FEE` is already added or the issue persists, contact [PayPal support](https://www.paypal.com/us/cshelp/technical). — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `PAYEE_NOT_ENABLED_FOR_CARD_PROCESSING`: Contact [PayPal support](https://www.paypal.com/us/cshelp/technical) to check the merchant or payee account configuration. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `PAYER_ACTION_REQUIRED`: Redirect the payer to the `'rel':'payer-action'` HATEOAS link before authorizing or capturing the order. Some payment methods require a webhook subscription to notify you of background payer actions before capture succeeds. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `PERMISSION_DENIED`: Make sure you have the correct permissions and scopes for the resource. If the resource ID belongs to another account, grant the necessary permissions. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `POSTAL_CODE_REQUIRED`: Add a postal code to the request and try again. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `REDIRECT_PAYER_FOR_ALTERNATE_FUNDING`: Redirect the payer to choose a different payment method. In PayPal, the payer can add a new payment method to their wallet or use a different card. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `REFERENCED_CARD_EXPIRED`: Ask the payer to update their card information. Otherwise, future attempts will fail. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `SHIPPING_ADDRESS_INVALID`: Fix the shipping address and try again. If using saved payment details, ask the payer for the correct shipping address. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview
- [parameter] `TOKEN_ID_NOT_FOUND`: Validate the payment token. - [parameter] `UNPROCESSABLE_ENTITY`: Contact [PayPal support](https://www.paypal.com/us/cshelp/technical) with the `debug_id` or `correlation_id` from the response header. - [parameter] `VALIDATION_ERROR`: Tell the payer the card number is incorrect and ask them to enter the correct number. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#common-errors-overview

- [definition] Forbidden (403) — The Orders v2 API returns a `403` status code when the API caller or payee does not have the required permissions for the request. The following sample shows a create order request that sets `items.category` to `DONATION` without the necessary permissions. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/overview#forbidden-403

## Unprocessable entity
<https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/unprocessable-entity>

- [definition] Unprocessable entity — PayPal cannot process your request because it is incorrect or fails business rules. The issues below can cause an `UNPROCESSABLE_ENTITY` error. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/unprocessable-entity#unprocessable-entity
- [concept] An UNPROCESSABLE_ENTITY error occurs when PayPal cannot process a request because it is incorrect or fails business rules. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/unprocessable-entity
- [fact] The AGREEMENT_ALREADY_CANCELLED error returns from the Billing Agreements API. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/unprocessable-entity
- [fact] The BILLING_AGREEMENT_NOT_FOUND error returns from the Billing Agreements API. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/unprocessable-entity
- [fact] The CANNOT_PAY_SELF error returns from the Billing Agreements API. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/unprocessable-entity
- [problem] The CARD_EXPIRED error is returned by the PayPal Orders v2 or Payments v1 APIs. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/unprocessable-entity
- [fact] The CURRENCY_NOT_SUPPORTED error returns from the Billing Agreements API. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/unprocessable-entity

## Validation error
<https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/validation-error>

- [definition] Validation error — You get a validation error when the API request contains invalid, missing, or incorrectly formatted data. This error can return from the [Orders v2](https://developer.paypal.com/docs/api/orders/v2/) or [Payments v1](https://developer.paypal.com/docs/api/payments/v1/) APIs. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/common-errors/validation-error#validation-error

- [definition] Handling payment failures with PayPal — Detect and respond to payment failures using PayPal's Orders v2 API and Subscriptions API. This guide explains common failure reasons, how to surface errors to buyers, best practices for retrying or recovering failed payments, and how to leverage PayPal's intelligent retry mechanism. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/handling-payment-failures-with-paypal#handling-payment-failures-with-paypal
- [definition] Common payment failure scenarios — Here are some of the most frequent failure reasons and how to handle them: — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/handling-payment-failures-with-paypal#common-payment-failure-scenarios
- [definition] Intelligent retries for subscriptions — PayPal's **intelligent retry** mechanism automatically attempts to recover failed subscription payments. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/handling-payment-failures-with-paypal#intelligent-retries-for-subscriptions
- [definition] Manual retries for subscriptions — In addition to intelligent retries, you can also manually retry failed subscription payments. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/handling-payment-failures-with-paypal#manual-retries-for-subscriptions
- [concept] Payment failures can occur for various reasons including declined cards, expired payment methods, insufficient funds, risk restrictions, or business validation errors. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/handling-payment-failures-with-paypal
- [fact] PayPal's Orders v2 and Subscriptions APIs return clear error codes and messages in their responses to indicate failure reasons. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/handling-payment-failures-with-paypal
- [concept] Some payment failures are asynchronous, meaning they might not be immediately apparent during the initial transaction response. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/handling-payment-failures-with-paypal
- [actionable] Always inspect responses from the Orders API (create, authorize, or capture endpoints) or the Subscriptions API (create, update, or activate subscription endpoints). — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/handling-payment-failures-with-paypal
- [actionable] Use `actions.restart()` in the Smart Buttons integration to restart the payment flow so the buyer can select a different funding source. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/handling-payment-failures-with-paypal
- [idea] Show clear, actionable error messages to buyers, such as 'Your card was declined. Please try another payment method.' — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/handling-payment-failures-with-paypal
- [actionable] For expired cards or exceeded attempts, ask the buyer to update their information or use a new card. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/handling-payment-failures-with-paypal
- [concept] PayPal's intelligent retry mechanism automatically attempts to recover failed subscription payments using a proprietary algorithm. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/handling-payment-failures-with-paypal
- [fact] The intelligent retry algorithm considers factors such as the buyer's payment history, risk signals, and bank availability to determine retry timing. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/handling-payment-failures-with-paypal
- [actionable] Merchants can configure retry schedules and monitor retry attempts in the PayPal dashboard. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/handling-payment-failures-with-paypal
- [idea] It is highly recommended to use PayPal Webhooks to track asynchronous payment failures reliably. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/handling-payment-failures-with-paypal
- [fact] The `PAYMENT.CAPTURE.DENIED` webhook event indicates a failed payment capture in the Orders API. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/handling-payment-failures-with-paypal
- [fact] The `BILLING.SUBSCRIPTION.PAYMENT.FAILED` webhook event indicates a failed subscription payment in the Subscriptions API. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/handling-payment-failures-with-paypal
- [actionable] You can retry payments directly from the PayPal dashboard for subscriptions. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/handling-payment-failures-with-paypal
- [actionable] Use the Subscriptions API to trigger a manual retry for failed subscription payments. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/handling-payment-failures-with-paypal
- [idea] Log all failed payment attempts with error codes and messages for troubleshooting purposes. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/handling-payment-failures-with-paypal
- [idea] Validate all order data before sending requests to avoid preventable business validation errors. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/handling-payment-failures-with-paypal
- [actionable] Check the PayPal status page to verify that PayPal services are operating normally during troubleshooting. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/handling-payment-failures-with-paypal
- [actionable] Use the PayPal Developer Dashboard to monitor your webhook events and API usage. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/api/troubleshooting/handling-payment-failures-with-paypal

## Apps, scopes, and credentials
<https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/apps-scopes-credentials>

- [definition] Apps, scopes, and credentials — PayPal Apps are registered apps within the PayPal Developer ecosystem that verify and authorize your software to interact with PayPal's services. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/apps-scopes-credentials#apps-scopes-and-credentials
- [definition] Understanding scopes — Scopes define your app's permissions and access levels to PayPal services. The scope field in the authentication response shows all available permissions for your app. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/apps-scopes-credentials#understanding-scopes
- [definition] PayPal API credentials — PayPal REST APIs use two types of credentials for authentication: — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/apps-scopes-credentials#paypal-api-credentials
- [definition] Getting credentials — Obtain credentials through the PayPal Developer Dashboard: — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/apps-scopes-credentials#getting-credentials
- [concept] PayPal Apps are registered entities that verify and authorize software to interact with PayPal services using unique credentials. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/apps-scopes-credentials
- [fact] Each app receives a client ID and client secret to establish a secure connection between the app and PayPal's payment processing network. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/apps-scopes-credentials
- [concept] Scopes define an app's permissions and access levels, with available permissions shown in the authentication response scope field. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/apps-scopes-credentials
- [fact] The scope https://uri.paypal.com/services/payments/payment/authcapture is used to process payments and captures. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/apps-scopes-credentials
- [fact] The scope https://uri.paypal.com/services/payments/refund allows processing refunds. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/apps-scopes-credentials
- [fact] The scope https://uri.paypal.com/services/vault/payment-tokens/creditcard is required to store credit card information. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/apps-scopes-credentials
- [fact] The scope https://uri.paypal.com/services/vault/payment-tokens/read enables managing stored credit cards. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/apps-scopes-credentials
- [fact] The scope https://uri.paypal.com/services/invoicing is used to create and manage invoices. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/apps-scopes-credentials
- [fact] The scope https://uri.paypal.com/services/subscriptions allows for subscription management. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/apps-scopes-credentials
- [fact] The scope https://uri.paypal.com/services/payments/payouts is used to send payouts. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/apps-scopes-credentials
- [fact] The scope https://uri.paypal.com/services/disputes/read-buyer permits reading buyer dispute information. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/apps-scopes-credentials
- [fact] The scope https://uri.paypal.com/services/disputes/update-seller allows updating seller dispute status. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/apps-scopes-credentials
- [fact] The scope https://uri.paypal.com/services/webhooks is used for webhook management. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/apps-scopes-credentials
- [fact] The openid scope is used for OpenID Connect authentication. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/apps-scopes-credentials
- [concept] Client ID is a public identifier safe to use in client-side code for basic payment buttons and card fields. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/apps-scopes-credentials
- [problem] Client Secret must be kept secure and used only server-side as it is a private key verifying the app for API calls. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/apps-scopes-credentials
- [actionable] New accounts automatically receive a Default Application with ready-to-use credentials in the PayPal Developer Dashboard. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/apps-scopes-credentials
- [actionable] Developers can create additional apps by selecting Create App within the Apps & Credentials section of the dashboard. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/apps-scopes-credentials
- [fact] The PayPal sandbox environment provides testing accounts to simulate real payment scenarios without processing actual transactions. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/apps-scopes-credentials

- [definition] Authenticate the SDK — Authenticate with a client ID or a client token. Most integrations should authenticate with a client ID. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration#authenticate-the-sdk
- [definition] Initialize the v6 SDK — Use `window.paypal.createInstance()` to initialize the SDK with your client ID or a client token for authentication. Also, use it to define the components you want to load and to manage other configurations like `locale` and `pageType`. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration#initialize-the-v6-sdk
- [definition] `window.paypal.createInstance(options)` — Use `window.paypal.createInstance` to initialize the PayPal SDK. This method configures the SDK for your specific integration needs and returns an SDK instance that you'll use to create payment sessions. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration#windowpaypalcreateinstanceoptions
- [definition] Recommended frontend setup — This is the recommended approach for most implementations. It includes all payment methods with eligibility logic and automatic fallback handling. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration#recommended-frontend-setup
- [definition] Example — The following is an example of what an `app.js` file might look like when implementing the recommended setup. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration#example
- [definition] Return order ID to SDK — The `createOrder()` function must return a promise that resolves to `{ orderId: "YOUR_ORDER_ID" }`. This is a key difference between v6 and previous versions of the SDK. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration#return-order-id-to-sdk
- [definition] Best practices — The following are best practices for integrating the v6 SDK. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration#best-practices
- [concept] The PayPal JavaScript SDK v6 is faster and more secure than previous versions, supporting standalone button and iframe-based integrations. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration
- [fact] For sandbox and testing environments, the SDK script URL must include the 'sandbox' parameter. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration
- [concept] Client ID authentication is recommended for most integrations as it acts as a static, safe-to-include application username. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration
- [fact] Direct merchants process payments into their own PayPal account, while partners process payments on behalf of other merchants. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration
- [problem] Client token authentication is required for Fastlane integrations; using it for other use cases is not recommended. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration
- [fact] A client token expires after 15 minutes and is bound to the domain for security, requiring regeneration when needed. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration
- [actionable] Partner calls must include PayPal-Auth-Assertion and PayPal-Partner-Attribution-Id headers when generating client tokens. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration
- [concept] The window.paypal.createInstance() method initializes the SDK, defines components to load, and manages configurations like locale and pageType. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration
- [fact] The 'clientId' and 'clientToken' parameters are mutually exclusive in the createInstance options. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration
- [concept] The components parameter accepts an array of SDK components to load, with 'paypal-payments' as the default. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration
- [fact] Venmo payments are supported via the venmo-payments component but are restricted to US only. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration
- [fact] The pageType parameter accepts values such as 'checkout', 'product-details', 'cart', 'mini-cart', and 'home' to optimize the payment experience. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration
- [fact] The clientMetadataId parameter is a unique identifier used for tracking and debugging, often generated using crypto.randomUUID(). — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration
- [fact] Partners must include the merchantId parameter when initializing the SDK instance, whereas direct merchants can omit it. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration
- [fact] The createInstance method returns a promise that resolves to an SDK instance object providing eligibility checking and session creation methods. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration
- [concept] The recommended frontend setup includes all payment methods with eligibility logic and automatic fallback handling. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration
- [fact] Eligibility checks determine available payment methods based on user location, currency, account status, and device type. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration
- [concept] Web components like <paypal-button>, <paypal-pay-later-button>, and <paypal-credit-button> are used for standard payment interactions. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration
- [problem] The createOrder() function must return a promise resolving to an object containing the orderId, which is a key difference from previous SDK versions. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration
- [idea] Never expose PayPal client secrets in frontend code; always obtain client tokens from a secure server. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration
- [problem] Passing up item totals from the browser is unsafe as this data can be manipulated by users. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration
- [idea] Always check payment eligibility before showing payment buttons to ensure a valid user experience. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration
- [idea] Handle popup blockers gracefully by using the presentationMode:auto option for payment flows. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration
- [fact] The locale parameter accepts BCP-47 language tags like 'en-US' or 'fr-FR', defaulting to the buyer's browser settings if unspecified. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration
- [fact] The BN_CODE for partnerAttributionId is issued by PayPal during the partner onboarding process. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/sdk/js/v6/configuration

- [definition] PayPal security guidelines for developers — Creating strong, secure payment systems keeps both your app and your customers' private information safe. Learn about and use PayPal's security guidelines, tools, and best practices when you integrate PayPal as a payment option to keep your customers and your app secure. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#paypal-security-guidelines-for-developers
- [definition] Overview — PayPal provides a robust set of APIs and SDKs for integrating payments into your applications. Security is a top priority, and PayPal enforces strict guidelines and provides tools to help developers protect sensitive data, authenticate securely, and prevent fraud. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#overview
- [definition] General security requirements and guidelines — The following security requirements and guidelines apply to all PayPal integrations and help protect both your application and your customers' sensitive payment information from unauthorized access and potential security threats. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#general-security-requirements-and-guidelines
- [definition] Use Open Authorization (OAuth) 2.0 for all API authentication — PayPal uses OAuth 2.0 as the standard authentication mechanism for all REST API integrations. OAuth 2.0 provides secure, token-based authentication, allowing applications to access PayPal APIs without exposing user credentials. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#use-open-authorization-oauth-20-for-all-api-authentication
- [definition] Always use HTTPS for all API and SDK requests — All communication with PayPal APIs and SDKs must use HTTPS to ensure data is encrypted in transit and protected from interception or tampering. HTTPS is required for PCI DSS compliance and is enforced by PayPal for all endpoints. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#always-use-https-for-all-api-and-sdk-requests
- [definition] Never expose your client secret or API credentials in client-side code — Your client secret and API credentials must always be kept confidential and used only on the server side. Exposing these credentials in client-side cod, such as JavaScript running in the browser, can lead to credential theft and unauthorized access to your PayPal account. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#never-expose-your-client-secret-or-api-credentials-in-client-side-code
- [definition] Store credentials securely using environment variables or secure vaults — Sensitive credentials should be stored in environment variables or secure vaults, not in source code or configuration files. This reduces the risk of accidental exposure through version control or code sharing. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#store-credentials-securely-using-environment-variables-or-secure-vaults
- [definition] Rotate API credentials regularly and remove unused credentials — Regularly rotating your API credentials and removing any that are no longer in use minimizes the risk if credentials are ever compromised. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#rotate-api-credentials-regularly-and-remove-unused-credentials
- [definition] Validate all webhook signatures to ensure authenticity — Always verify webhook signatures using PayPal's verification API before processing events. This ensures the event is genuine and untampered, protecting your application from spoofed or malicious notifications. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#validate-all-webhook-signatures-to-ensure-authenticity
- [definition] Keep all dependencies, SDKs, and libraries up to date — Regularly update all dependencies, SDKs, and libraries to patch security vulnerabilities and benefit from the latest security improvements. Outdated software can expose your application to known exploits. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#keep-all-dependencies-sdks-and-libraries-up-to-date
- [definition] Use Transport Layer Security (TLS) 1.2 or higher — Ensure your servers and clients support TLS 1.2 or higher for all communications with PayPal. TLS provides strong encryption and is required for PCI DSS compliance. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#use-transport-layer-security-tls-12-or-higher
- [definition] Enable two-factor authentication (2FA) on your PayPal account — Enable 2FA to add an extra layer of security to your PayPal account and developer dashboard. 2FA helps prevent unauthorized access, even if your password is compromised. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#enable-two-factor-authentication-2fa-on-your-paypal-account
- [definition] Monitor and log all payment and API activity for suspicious behavior — Implement logging and monitoring for all payment and API activity to detect and respond to suspicious or unauthorized actions. Monitoring helps you identify fraud, abuse, or operational issues quickly. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#monitor-and-log-all-payment-and-api-activity-for-suspicious-behavior
- [definition] Follow the principle of least privilege for API credentials — Grant only the minimum permissions necessary for each API credential to reduce risk if credentials are compromised. Use separate credentials for different environments and roles. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#follow-the-principle-of-least-privilege-for-api-credentials
- [definition] Do not log sensitive information such as access tokens or payment details — Never log sensitive data such as access tokens, client secrets, or full payment details to prevent accidental exposure. Logging sensitive data can lead to data breaches and non-compliance with PCI DSS. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#do-not-log-sensitive-information-such-as-access-tokens-or-payment-details
- [definition] Integration-specific security guidelines — PayPal offers several integration paths, each with unique security considerations beyond the general guidelines. The following sections outline specific security requirements, best practices, and code examples for API, SDK, and webhook integrations. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#integration-specific-security-guidelines
- [definition] API integration path — The API integration path is for developers who interact directly with PayPal's REST APIs from their server-side applications. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#api-integration-path
- [definition] SDK integration path — The SDK integration path is for developers who use PayPal's JavaScript SDK to embed payment buttons and checkout flows directly into their web applications. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#sdk-integration-path
- [definition] Webhook and notification integration — Webhook and notification integration is essential for handling asynchronous events from PayPal, such as payment completions, disputes, or subscription updates. This path ensures your server can securely receive and process notifications and that your system trusts only genuine PayPal events. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#webhook-and-notification-integration
- [definition] Security testing guidelines — Testing your security implementation is critical to ensure your integration is robust against various threats and edge cases. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#security-testing-guidelines
- [definition] PayPal security tools — PayPal offers a suite of security tools and features to help secure your integration. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#paypal-security-tools
- [concept] PayPal uses OAuth 2.0 as the standard authentication mechanism for all REST API integrations, providing secure token-based access without exposing user credentials. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#general-security-requirements-and-guidelines
- [actionable] Your server must send a POST request to the PayPal OAuth endpoint with client credentials to obtain a short-lived access token for subsequent API calls. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#general-security-requirements-and-guidelines
- [fact] All communication with PayPal APIs and SDKs must use HTTPS, which is enforced by PayPal and required for PCI DSS compliance. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#general-security-requirements-and-guidelines
- [problem] Exposing client secrets or API credentials in client-side code like JavaScript can lead to credential theft and unauthorized account access. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#general-security-requirements-and-guidelines
- [idea] Store sensitive credentials in environment variables or secure vaults rather than in source code or configuration files to prevent accidental exposure via version control. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#general-security-requirements-and-guidelines
- [actionable] Use the developer dashboard to generate, revoke, and monitor API credentials as part of a regular rotation schedule. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#general-security-requirements-and-guidelines
- [concept] Webhook signatures must always be verified using PayPal's verification API to ensure events are genuine and untampered before processing. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#general-security-requirements-and-guidelines
- [fact] PayPal endpoints require Transport Layer Security (TLS) 1.2 or higher and do not support older, less secure protocols. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#general-security-requirements-and-guidelines
- [idea] Enable two-factor authentication (2FA) on your PayPal account and developer dashboard to add an extra layer of security against compromised passwords. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#general-security-requirements-and-guidelines
- [problem] Logging sensitive information such as access tokens, client secrets, or full payment details can lead to data breaches and non-compliance with PCI DSS. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#general-security-requirements-and-guidelines
- [idea] Follow the principle of least privilege by granting only minimum necessary permissions for each API credential and using separate credentials for different environments. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#general-security-requirements-and-guidelines
- [fact] PayPal provides tools in the developer dashboard to manage, rotate, and monitor API credentials. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#general-security-requirements-and-guidelines
- [concept] Security testing ensures an integration is robust against threats and edge cases. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#security-testing-guidelines
- [fact] Comprehensive response handling testing is covered in the Handling API responses guide. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#security-testing-guidelines
- [actionable] Test with expired access tokens to verify token refresh logic works correctly. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#security-testing-guidelines
- [actionable] Attempt operations with insufficient permissions to ensure proper access control. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#security-testing-guidelines
- [actionable] Verify webhook signature verification correctly rejects unsigned or improperly signed events. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#security-testing-guidelines
- [fact] Sensitive data must not be logged in application logs during testing. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#security-testing-guidelines
- [fact] Error messages displayed to end-users must not expose sensitive information. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#security-testing-guidelines
- [actionable] Test that client-side code never receives or processes sensitive credentials. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#security-testing-guidelines
- [idea] Use PayPal's negative testing tools to simulate various security-related errors. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#security-testing-guidelines
- [fact] PayPal's negative testing tools are available at https://developer.paypal.com/tools/sandbox/negative-testing/request-headers/. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#security-testing-guidelines
- [actionable] Test your implementation with different network conditions such as timeouts and dropped connections. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#security-testing-guidelines
- [fact] Your TLS configuration must meet PayPal's security requirements. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/how-to/security-guidelines#security-testing-guidelines

## Agent tools reference

- [snippet] Additional tools for remote MCP server: { — `{` — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/agent-tools-ref#additional-tools-for-remote-mcp-server
- [parameter] `create_product`: Description=Create a new product in the PayPal catalog.; Parameters=`name` (string, required): The name of the product. <br /><br /> `type` (string, required, enum): The type of the product. Allowed values are `'PHYSICAL'`, `'DIGITAL'`, `'SERVICE'`.; Sample Prompt=Create a new product with the name `{product_name}` of type `{product_type}`. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/agent-tools-ref#catalog-management
- [parameter] `list_product`: Description=List products from the PayPal catalog. Pagination options are available.; Parameters=`page` (number, optional): The specific page number to retrieve. The default behavior is to go to the first page. <br /><br /> `page_size` (number, optional): The maximum number of products to return per page. The default behavior is to use a system-defined limit.; Sample Prompt=List… — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/agent-tools-ref#catalog-management
- [parameter] `show_product_details`: Description=Show details of a specific product from the PayPal catalog.; Parameters=`product_id` (string, required): The ID of the product to retrieve.; Sample Prompt=Show the details for product id `{product_id}`. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/agent-tools-ref#catalog-management

- [definition] Remote MCP server — If you don't want to install MCP server locally, the other option is to use the remotely hosted MCP server. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/mcp-quickstart#remote-mcp-server
