- [definition] Example: Create an invoice using MCP server tools — Using your favorite MCP client with the MCP server to create invoices offers several advantages over creating invoices in a more traditional way. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/mcp-quickstart#example-create-an-invoice-using-mcp-server-tools
- [concept] Model Context Protocol (MCP) makes it easier to manage and send information to models, helping them work better in specific contexts. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/mcp-quickstart
- [fact] PayPal provides two ways for merchants to set up the MCP server: running it locally or using it remotely. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/mcp-quickstart
- [concept] Running the MCP server locally enables developers to download, install, and run the server on their own machine. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/mcp-quickstart
- [concept] Using the remote MCP server allows users to continue tasks across devices with a single login after authentication. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/mcp-quickstart
- [fact] The examples in this documentation use Claude as the MCP client, but any MCP client like Cursor or Cline can be used. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/mcp-quickstart
- [problem] To run the MCP server locally, you must have Node.js v18 or later installed. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/mcp-quickstart
- [actionable] Locate the external tools or connectors section in your MCP client's configuration and add the PayPal connector configuration. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/mcp-quickstart
- [fact] In Claude, the configuration is added to `~/Claude/claude_desktop_config.json`. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/mcp-quickstart
- [actionable] Replace `YOUR_PAYPAL_ACCESS_TOKEN` with your actual PayPal access token and set `PAYPAL_ENVIRONMENT` to `SANDBOX` for testing or `PRODUCTION` for production. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/mcp-quickstart
- [actionable] You can set the `PAYPAL_ACCESS_TOKEN` as an environment variable or pass it as an argument using `--access-token` in `args`. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/mcp-quickstart
- [actionable] To test your integration, quit and restart the MCP client to apply changes, then ask it to perform a supported task like listing PayPal invoices. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/mcp-quickstart
- [fact] When developing or testing in the sandbox environment, you must point to the sandbox endpoint `https://mcp.sandbox.paypal.com`. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/mcp-quickstart
- [fact] For a live site in production, use the PayPal client credentials from the PayPal Developer Dashboard. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/mcp-quickstart
- [actionable] To connect to the remote MCP server, save your configuration file and restart your MCP client, which will redirect you to the PayPal login page. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/mcp-quickstart
- [actionable] Log into PayPal on the login page and authorize the client to work with the MCP server to complete the connection. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/mcp-quickstart
- [problem] If you have trouble connecting after completing the procedure, try clearing the files in `~/.mcp-auth` by running `rm -rf ~/.mcp-auth`. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/mcp-quickstart
- [problem] Windows users with Cursor might encounter a known issue when connecting to the MCP server remotely due to emerging technology challenges. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/mcp-quickstart
- [fact] The complete catalog of tools is available in both local and remote MCP servers. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/mcp-quickstart
- [idea] Using the MCP server allows merchants to process multiple invoice requests via natural language using drive-system or file-system connectors. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/mcp-quickstart
- [fact] In a production environment for SSE, replace the sandbox URL with `https://mcp.paypal.com/sse`. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/mcp-quickstart
- [fact] In a production environment for streamable HTTP, replace the sandbox URL with `https://mcp.paypal.com/http`. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/mcp-quickstart
- [concept] Streamable HTTP allows servers to send data in chunks so customers can receive responses immediately without long delays. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/mcp-quickstart

## Best practices for prompts
<https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices>

- [parameter] Be clear about what to do and how to do it.: Put the action first ("Create an invoice…"), then supply parameter values in the order the tool expects. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices#best-practices-for-prompts
- [parameter] Be specific.: Include every required field, such as `currency` and `invoice_id`. Unclear input can cause failure. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices#best-practices-for-prompts
- [parameter] Separate complex tasks into simple ones.: If you want to create a checkout sequence, you might have 3 subtasks: <br /> - Create order. <br /> - Pay order. <br /> - Create shipment. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices#best-practices-for-prompts
- [parameter] Encourage the agent to "think.": Begin an analytic request with a statement like "Think step-by-step before calling the tool…". This can help the agent to avoid making bad calls. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices#best-practices-for-prompts
- [parameter] Protect personally identifiable information (PII).: Never embed card numbers, personal financial information like social security numbers (SSNs), passwords, or secrets in prompts. The agent toolkit will reject this information, and you might violate the Payment Card Industry Data Security Standard (PCI DSS). — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices#best-practices-for-prompts
- [definition] Best practices for prompts — Build effective prompts that unlock the full potential of PayPal AI agents with these tips. To start building AI tools using PayPal'a agent toolkit, see the [agent toolkit quickstart guide](/developer/tools/ai/agent-toolkit-quickstart/). — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices#best-practices-for-prompts
- [definition] Multistep agent prompt examples — The following examples provide ideas for how to form multistep prompts for some standard use cases. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices#multistep-agent-prompt-examples
- [definition] Invoice a client — To invoice a client, you might have steps like these: — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices#invoice-a-client
- [definition] Create an online order — For an online order with shipping, you might use these steps in your prompts: — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices#create-an-online-order
- [definition] Sell a subscription — Subscriptions are a type of product, which come with specific parameters. To sell subscriptions, you might complete these tasks using your agent tools: — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices#sell-a-subscription
- [definition] Evaluate and resolve disputes — To manage disputes, you might use prompts like these: — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices#evaluate-and-resolve-disputes
- [definition] Add and confirm the details for a new product — When you add a new product, you'll want to confirm that the product appears where and how it should. These prompts could do that: — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices#add-and-confirm-the-details-for-a-new-product
- [definition] Create an invoice with a QR code — This example shows how to invoice a client and use a QR code for on-site payment. To complete this sort of process, you might use the following tools and steps: — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices#create-an-invoice-with-a-qr-code
- [definition] Fix an invoice error — Correcting an invoice error might require steps like these: — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices#fix-an-invoice-error
- [definition] Reconcile a transaction — To reconcile a transaction, compare the order details to the transaction itself: — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices#reconcile-a-transaction
- [definition] Cancel a subscription — If a user wants to cancel a subscription, you might need to check the terms of their subscription. To do that, you could use these steps: — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices#cancel-a-subscription
- [definition] See also — For more information about using AI with PayPal, see [PayPal's AI resources for developers](/developer/tools/ai/ai-intro/). — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices#see-also
- [concept] Multistep prompts for PayPal AI agents consist of sequential tool calls to handle complex workflows. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices
- [idea] To invoice a client, chain create_invoice, send_invoice, and send_invoice_reminder steps. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices
- [fact] The create_invoice tool accepts parameters for recipient email, service description, duration, and hourly rate. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices
- [idea] For online orders with shipping, chain create_order, pay_order, and create_shipment_tracking steps. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices
- [fact] The create_shipment_tracking tool requires a tracking number (e.g., 1Z999AA10123456784) and carrier name (e.g., UPS). — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices
- [idea] Selling subscriptions involves creating a product, defining a subscription plan, and then creating the subscription. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices
- [fact] The create_product tool supports a type parameter such as DIGITAL for digital products. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices
- [fact] The create_subscription_plan tool defines frequency (e.g., MONTH) and price per period. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices
- [idea] To manage disputes, chain list_disputes, get_dispute, and accept_dispute_claim steps. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices
- [fact] The list_disputes tool can filter by status, such as OPEN. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices
- [idea] When adding a new product, verify its appearance using list_product and show_product_details steps. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices
- [fact] The list_product tool supports pagination parameters like page and page_size. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices
- [fact] The create_product tool accepts a type parameter such as PHYSICAL for physical products. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices
- [idea] To invoice with on-site payment, chain create_invoice, send_invoice, and generate_invoice_qr_code steps. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices
- [fact] The generate_invoice_qr_code tool generates a QR code for a specific invoice ID. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices
- [idea] To fix an invoice error, cancel the SENT invoice and create a corrected one. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices
- [fact] The cancel_sent_invoice tool targets invoices with a status of SENT. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices
- [idea] To reconcile a transaction, compare order details to the transaction using list_transaction and get_order steps. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices
- [fact] The list_transaction tool can filter transactions by a time range, such as the last 14 days. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices
- [idea] To cancel a subscription, first check terms using show_subscription_details before executing cancel_subscription. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/tools/ai/prompt-best-practices

## Upgrade from v5
<https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6>

- [snippet] SDK script: <script src="https://www.paypal.com/sdk/js?client-id=YOUR_CLIENT_ID&currency=USD"></script> — `<script src="https://www.paypal.com/sdk/js?client-id=YOUR_CLIENT_ID&currency=USD"></script>` — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6#sdk-script
- [snippet] SDK script: <script async src="https://www.sandbox.paypal.com/web-sdk/v6/core" onload="onPayPalLoaded()"></script> — `<script async src="https://www.sandbox.paypal.com/web-sdk/v6/core" onload="onPayPalLoaded()"></scrip` — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6#sdk-script
- [snippet] Authentication: Client ID (Recommended): const sdkInstance = await window.paypal.createInstance({ — `const sdkInstance = await window.paypal.createInstance({` — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6#authentication-client-id-recommended
- [snippet] Authentication: Client ID (Recommended): async function getBrowserSafeClientToken() { — `// Fetch browser-safe client token from your server` — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6#authentication-client-id-recommended
- [snippet] Render payment buttons: paypal.Buttons({ ... }).render('#paypal-button-container'); — `paypal.Buttons({ ... }).render('#paypal-button-container');` — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6#render-payment-buttons
- [snippet] Render payment buttons: <paypal-button id="paypal-button" type="pay" hidden></paypal-button> — `<paypal-button id="paypal-button" type="pay" hidden></paypal-button>` — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6#render-payment-buttons
- [snippet] Manage orders and callbacks: paypal.Buttons({ — `paypal.Buttons({` — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6#manage-orders-and-callbacks
- [snippet] Manage orders and callbacks: async function createOrder() { — `async function createOrder() {` — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6#manage-orders-and-callbacks
- [snippet] Handle sessions and button events: const paypalPaymentSession = sdkInstance.createPayPalOneTimePaymentSession({ — `const paypalPaymentSession = sdkInstance.createPayPalOneTimePaymentSession({` — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6#handle-sessions-and-button-events
- [snippet] Check eligibility: const paymentMethods = await sdkInstance.findEligibleMethods({ currencyCode: "USD" }); — `const paymentMethods = await sdkInstance.findEligibleMethods({ currencyCode: "USD" });` — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6#check-eligibility
- [parameter] **SDK script URL**: v5=You load the SDK with `https://www.paypal.com/sdk/js?client-id=YOUR_CLIENT_ID&currency=USD`.; v6=You load the SDK with `https://www.sandbox.paypal.com/web-sdk/v6/core`. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6#compare-v5-and-v6
- [parameter] **Global `paypal` object**: v5=All features are available from the global object.; v6=You call `createInstance`, which sets up the SDK. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6#compare-v5-and-v6
- [parameter] **Button rendering**: v5=You render buttons with `paypal.Buttons({ ... }).render()`.; v6=You use `<paypal-button/>` with an event listener and call `session.start()`. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6#compare-v5-and-v6
- [parameter] **Callbacks**: v5=You pass callbacks into `paypal.Buttons()`.; v6=You pass callbacks into a checkout session, such as `createPayPalOneTimePaymentSession`. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6#compare-v5-and-v6
- [parameter] **Venmo support**: v5=Venmo is included automatically in the smart stack.; v6=You enable Venmo with the `venmo-payments` component. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6#compare-v5-and-v6
- [definition] Upgrade from v5 — Use this guide to upgrade your PayPal integration from the JavaScript SDK v5 to the JavaScript SDK v6. The v6 SDK introduces custom elements for UI, uses payment sessions to manage checkout flows, and offers flexible authentication with a client ID or client token. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6#upgrade-from-v5
- [definition] Compare v5 and v6 — The following table shows how the v5 and v6 SDKs are different. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6#compare-v5-and-v6
- [definition] Migration overview — The following sections explain the main changes and concepts you need to consider when upgrading from v5 to v6 of the PayPal JavaScript SDK. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6#migration-overview
- [definition] SDK script — In v5, you load the SDK using a script tag with the client ID and configuration directly in the URL. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6#sdk-script
- [definition] Authentication: Client ID (Recommended) — v5 uses your client ID in the script URL. v6 uses your client ID in the `createInstance` call when initializing the SDK (recommended for most integrations): — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6#authentication-client-id-recommended
- [definition] Render payment buttons — In v5, you render PayPal buttons directly through `paypal.Buttons().render()`. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6#render-payment-buttons
- [definition] Manage orders and callbacks — In v5, you define `createOrder` and `onApprove` inside the button configuration. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6#manage-orders-and-callbacks
- [definition] Handle sessions and button events — In v5, you handle button logic inside the `paypal.Buttons` configuration. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6#handle-sessions-and-button-events
- [definition] Check eligibility — In v5, eligibility checks are built in internally before rendering each button. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6#check-eligibility
- [concept] The v6 SDK introduces custom elements for UI and uses payment sessions to manage checkout flows. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6
- [fact] In v5, the SDK script tag includes the client ID and configuration directly in the URL. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6
- [fact] In v6, the SDK core script is loaded without the client ID or any configuration in the URL. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6
- [actionable] Initialize the SDK using a `createInstance` call with your client ID for recommended authentication. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6
- [fact] Client token authentication is required for specific features like PayPal payment vaulting or Fastlane. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6
- [concept] In v6, you use custom elements to render payment buttons and control visibility in JavaScript. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6
- [fact] In v5, eligibility checks for payment methods are built in internally before rendering each button. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6
- [actionable] Check eligibility in v6 using the `findEligibleMethods` method or by calling the server-side API. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6
- [concept] In v6, you define `createOrder` and `onApprove` as standalone functions and pass them to the payment session. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6
- [fact] In v5, button logic including callbacks is defined inside the `paypal.Buttons` configuration. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6
- [idea] Load only the components you need for your integration in v6. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6
- [fact] The v6 SDK offers flexible authentication using either a client ID or a client token. — https://docs.paypal.ai/developer/upgrade/sdk/js/v5-v6

## Agentic commerce services
<https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/overview>

- [definition] Agentic commerce services — PayPal's agentic commerce services help create AI-powered shopping experiences, so customers can shop using everyday language. These services help customers find products, manage their shopping carts, and complete purchases easily across different online stores and platforms. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/overview#agentic-commerce-services
- [definition] Store Sync — Store Sync makes your products discoverable by AI shopping assistants and lets them place orders directly into your existing order management systems. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/overview#store-sync
- [definition] Agent Ready — With small changes to your current payment setup, you can accept payments through AI assistants, like a store's shopping bot within ChatGPT. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/overview#agent-ready
- [definition] Next steps — Use these pages to unlock the capabilities of PayPal's agentic commerce services: — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/overview#next-steps
- [concept] Agentic commerce services enable AI-powered shopping experiences where customers use everyday language to find products, manage carts, and complete purchases. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/overview
- [fact] Merchants must complete a specific form to contact PayPal's AI team and request access to agentic commerce services. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/overview
- [concept] Store Sync allows products to be discoverable by AI shopping assistants, enabling them to place orders directly into existing order management systems. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/overview
- [fact] Store Sync supports product catalog integration and cart operations to maintain merchant control over brand appearance and customer communications. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/overview
- [concept] Agent Ready enables merchants to accept payments through AI assistants with minimal changes to their current payment setup. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/overview
- [fact] With Agent Ready, PayPal handles security and compatibility across different platforms, eliminating the need for separate connections for each AI platform. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/overview
- [idea] Merchants should connect to PayPal once via Store Sync and Agent Ready to reach customers across multiple AI shopping platforms. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/overview
- [fact] Agent Ready supports both supervised and automatic shopping scenarios as the business grows. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/overview
- [actionable] Use the PayPal extension from the Visual Studio Marketplace to access code snippets and API references for agentic commerce services. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/overview
- [concept] Customers can interact with AI assistants to find products, manage shopping carts, and complete purchases without browsing websites. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/overview
- [fact] Store Sync partners include Wix, Cymbio, Commerce (BigCommerce & Feedonomics), and Shopware for product listing connections. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/overview

- [definition] Update a cart — To update an existing cart by replacing its contents with the provided data, use `PUT /merchant-cart/{id}`. - [concept] The Cart API provides three core endpoints to handle the complete cart lifecycle for agentic shopping flows. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/braintree-integration
- [fact] The POST /merchant-cart endpoint creates a new cart. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/braintree-integration
- [fact] The PUT /merchant-cart/{id} endpoint updates an existing cart. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/braintree-integration
- [fact] The POST /merchant-cart/{id}/checkout endpoint completes the checkout process. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/braintree-integration
- [concept] Every API request must include a PayPal-supplied JWT in the authorization header. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/braintree-integration
- [fact] Tokens contain the merchant ID and permissions for merchant identification. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/braintree-integration
- [actionable] Verify the token signature using PayPal's public keys found at https://www.paypal.ai/.well-known/jwks.json. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/braintree-integration
- [actionable] Parse the Authorization header using the format "Bearer <token>". — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/braintree-integration
- [fact] You must validate the token expiration as part of security verification. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/braintree-integration
- [concept] Agentic checkout requires Braintree credentials, specifically merchant_id and tokenization_key. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/braintree-integration
- [fact] PayPal uses your Braintree credentials to create a token during cart creation; you do not need to return a token in the request. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/braintree-integration
- [actionable] Use POST /merchant-cart to create a new shopping cart with items and customer information. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/braintree-integration
- [problem] The PUT endpoint performs a complete replacement of the cart, removing or resetting any fields not included in the request. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/braintree-integration
- [idea] Include all current cart data when making changes via PUT to avoid unintended data loss. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/braintree-integration
- [fact] The POST /merchant-cart/{id}/checkout endpoint finalizes the purchase by processing payment using the specified method. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/braintree-integration
- [fact] Within the checkout endpoint, PayPal passes a Braintree nonce that can be used to create a transaction. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/braintree-integration

- [parameter] `type`: Cause classification: `MISSING_FIELD`, `INVALID_DATA`, `BUSINESS_RULE` — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling#validation-issues

- [parameter] `PAYMENT_ERROR`: Payment limits, currency support, processor issues — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling#error-categories

- [parameter] `BUSINESS_RULE_ERROR`: Account restrictions, compliance, regional limits — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling#error-categories
- [parameter] `MISSING_FIELD`: Need more info (address, checkout fields) — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling#error-types
- [parameter] `INVALID_DATA`: Data validation failed — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling#error-types
- [parameter] `BUSINESS_RULE`: Business logic violation, such as inventory issues — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling#error-types
- [parameter] `ACCEPT_NEW_PRICE`: Use Case=Price increases; Auto-applicable=No — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling#resolution-action-types

- [parameter] `SUGGEST_ALTERNATIVE`: Use Case=Out of stock items; Auto-applicable=Yes — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling#resolution-action-types
- [parameter] `UPDATE_ADDRESS`: Use Case=Address validation; Auto-applicable=Yes (if correction available) — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling#resolution-action-types
- [parameter] `REMOVE_ITEM`: Use Case=Restriction violations; Auto-applicable=Depends on item importance — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling#resolution-action-types
- [parameter] `SPLIT_ORDER`: Use Case=Payment limits; Auto-applicable=Yes — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling#resolution-action-types
- [parameter] `CONTACT_SUPPORT`: Use Case=Complex issues; Auto-applicable=No — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling#resolution-action-types
- [parameter] `RETRY_LATER`: Use Case=Temporary failures; Auto-applicable=Yes — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling#resolution-action-types
- [definition] Response handling — To handle Cart API responses effectively and provide AI agents with the context they need for intelligent decision-making, understand the status fields and error patterns throughout the cart lifecycle. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling#response-handling
- [definition] Cart status fields — The PayPal Cart API uses 3 key status indicators that work together to communicate cart state and validation issues. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling#cart-status-fields
- [definition] Business outcome status — The merchant-controlled `status` value indicates the current state of the cart. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling#business-outcome-status
- [definition] Validation status — The `validation_status` value indicates whether the cart contents and data are valid for checkout. A valid transaction meets these requirements: — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling#validation-status
- [definition] Validation issues — The `validation_issues` array communicates business logic problems that prevent cart completion but don't require HTTP error codes. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling#validation-issues
- [definition] Error handling — Effective error handling is critical for maintaining a smooth shopping experience. The API provides rich error context that enables AI agents to resolve issues automatically or guide customers to resolution. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling#error-handling
- [definition] Error categories — The Cart API organizes validation issues into logical categories that help AI agents understand the nature of problems and apply appropriate resolution strategies. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling#error-categories
- [definition] Error types — Each validation issue includes a type field that classifies the underlying cause. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling#error-types
- [definition] Common error pattern — Most validation errors follow this structure with clear user messaging and actionable options. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling#common-error-pattern
- [definition] Error handling implementation strategy — When implementing error handling for the PayPal Cart API, use the following guidelines to determine appropriate HTTP status codes and error responses. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling#error-handling-implementation-strategy
- [definition] Next steps — Continue by [looking at integration examples](/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/integration-use-cases). — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling#next-steps
- [concept] The PayPal Cart API uses three key status indicators: business outcome status, validation status, and validation issues to communicate cart state. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling
- [fact] A valid transaction requires all items to be available with current pricing, complete required information, no business rule violations, and an empty validation_issues array. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling
- [concept] The validation_issues array communicates business logic problems that prevent cart completion without requiring HTTP error codes. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling
- [actionable] When returning a 422 Unprocessable Entity, include a business_context field in the details[] array that follows the validation issue structure. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling
- [idea] Include detailed information and resolution options in validation errors to support AI agents in understanding problems and fixing them. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling
- [fact] Use HTTP 200 OK for business logic issues such as inventory changes, price changes, or payment processing issues that can be resolved. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling
- [fact] Use HTTP 422 Unprocessable Entity when a validation issue prevents the creation of a cart, such as an OUT_OF_STOCK issue. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling
- [fact] Use HTTP 400 Bad Request for malformed requests including invalid JSON, missing required fields like the items array, or invalid cart ID formats. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling
- [fact] Use HTTP 404 Not Found when a well-formed request references a resource that does not exist, such as a cart ID not found in the merchant's system. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling
- [fact] Use HTTP 500 Internal Server Error for unexpected server-side issues that prevent processing. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling
- [actionable] For invalid cart ID formats, return a 400 response with detailed validation information matching the expected pattern like CART-[A-Z0-9]+. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling
- [idea] When designing error responses, provide rich context including customer-friendly messages for end users and technical details for developers. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling
- [idea] Always include resolution guidance, cost impact, and time estimates in error messages to enable intelligent resolution by AI agents. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling
- [concept] The Cart API organizes validation issues into logical categories and types to help AI agents understand the nature of problems and apply resolution strategies. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling
- [idea] Focus error handling on two essential patterns: fix issues automatically if possible, or show customers how to fix them if not. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/agentic-commerce/store-sync/your-api/set-up-your-api/response-handling

## Choose integration option
<https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/choose-integration-option>

- [parameter] **Who should use it**: Resolution Center=Best for businesses handling a small volume of disputes or those who want a no-code solution without technical integration needs.; Disputes API=Best for businesses handling a high volume of disputes or those who want to automate dispute handling. This works well for businesses with developers and technical expertise. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/choose-integration-option#choose-integration-option
- [parameter] **How it works**: Resolution Center=You log into your PayPal business account, go to the Resolution Center, and manage disputes manually. You can communicate with buyers, provide supporting evidence, issue refunds, or escalate cases.; Disputes API=You integrate PayPal's Disputes API into your system to programmatically manage disputes. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/choose-integration-option#choose-integration-option
- [parameter] **Key benefits**: Resolution Center=<ul><li>No development effort required</li><li>Easy to use with faster rollout</li><li>Intuitive dashboard</li></ul>; Disputes API=<ul><li>Enables automation at scale</li><li>Streamlines workflows for faster resolution</li><li>Seamless integration with your internal tools and systems</li></ul> — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/choose-integration-option#choose-integration-option
- [definition] Choose integration option — PayPal's dispute management provides two integration options to help you manage disputes seamlessly. Each option addresses different business needs based on dispute volume, automation requirements, and technical capabilities. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/choose-integration-option#choose-integration-option
- [concept] PayPal's dispute management provides two integration options to help manage disputes seamlessly. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/choose-integration-option
- [fact] The two available options are Resolution Center and Disputes API. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/choose-integration-option
- [concept] Each option addresses different business needs based on dispute volume, automation requirements, and technical capabilities. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/choose-integration-option
- [concept] Resolution Center is a web-based tool in your PayPal business account dashboard that lets you view, respond, and resolve disputes. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/choose-integration-option
- [concept] Disputes API is a RESTful API that lets you integrate PayPal dispute management into your application or internal system. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/choose-integration-option

For example, provide refund ID for `PROOF_OF_REFUND` and tracking information for `PROOF_OF_FULFILLMENT`. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#appeal-dispute
- [definition] Use Disputes API — You can use the PayPal Disputes API to handle and resolve disputes programmatically throughout their lifecycle. This guide shows you how to gather dispute information, respond based on lifecycle stages, and automate dispute handling using webhooks. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#use-disputes-api
- [definition] Prerequisites — Before you use the Disputes API, ensure you: — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#prerequisites
- [definition] Understand dispute lifecycle stages — Every dispute goes through defined stages from creation to resolution. Understanding them helps you determine the required [actions and responses at each stage](#respond-to-a-dispute). — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#understand-dispute-lifecycle-stages
- [definition] Accelerated Response — Accelerated Response enhances the inquiry stage. As a merchant, you can submit supporting documentation within 10 days after the inquiry starts. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#accelerated-response
- [definition] Gather dispute information — You can use the Disputes API to gather information about disputes associated with your account. The following sections describe how to [list all disputes](#list-disputes) and [retrieve details of a specific dispute](#show-dispute-details). — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#gather-dispute-information
- [definition] List disputes — Use a <a href="/developer/how-to/api/get-started#2-get-an-access-token">valid access token</a> and make a `GET` call to the `/v1/customer/disputes` endpoint to view all the disputes associated with an account. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#list-disputes
- [definition] Show dispute details — Use a <a href="/developer/how-to/api/get-started#2-get-an-access-token">valid access token</a> and make a `GET` call to the `/v1/customer/disputes/{ID}` endpoint to retrieve details of a specific dispute. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#show-dispute-details
- [definition] Respond to a dispute — After gathering dispute information, you can determine the appropriate response action using the following key parameters from the [Show dispute details](#show-dispute-details) response: — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#respond-to-a-dispute
- [definition] Send message to other party — You can send a message to the other party during the inquiry stage to share information that helps resolve the dispute. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#send-message-to-other-party
- [definition] Make offer to resolve dispute — You can make an offer to the buyer to resolve the dispute. To determine which offer types are available for a specific dispute, check the `allowed_response_options.make_offer.offer_types` parameter in the [Show dispute details](#show-dispute-details) response. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#make-offer-to-resolve-dispute
- [definition] Accept claim — You can accept liability for a dispute by accepting the claim. This closes the dispute in the buyer's favor, and PayPal automatically refunds the buyer. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#accept-claim
- [definition] Provide evidence — You can provide evidence for a dispute to support your case. PayPal requests specific evidence based on the dispute reason. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#provide-evidence
- [definition] Escalate dispute to claim — If either party is unsatisfied with the other party's response during the inquiry stage, they can escalate the dispute to a claim. The dispute then moves to the claim stage. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#escalate-dispute-to-claim
- [definition] Provide supporting information — You can provide additional supporting information for a case under PayPal's review to help with the review process. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#provide-supporting-information
- [definition] Appeal dispute — You can appeal a dispute that was resolved in the buyer's favor. To determine if a dispute is eligible for appeal, check whether the HATEOAS link for appeal is present in the [Show dispute details](#show-dispute-details) response. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#appeal-dispute
- [definition] Acknowledge returned item — For SNAD disputes with the `MERCHANDISE_OR_SERVICE_NOT_AS_DESCRIBED` reason, you can [make an offer to resolve the dispute](#make-offer-to-resolve-dispute) by offering a full refund in exchange for returning the item. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#acknowledge-returned-item
- [definition] Common issues — When calling the Disputes API, you may see these common errors: — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#common-issues
- [definition] Use webhooks to monitor dispute events — Webhook events are external events that your app does not know about unless it receives event notifications. For example, a new dispute raised or resolved is a webhook event. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#use-webhooks-to-monitor-dispute-events
- [concept] The PayPal Disputes API allows users to handle and resolve disputes programmatically throughout their lifecycle. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api
- [actionable] Gather dispute information using the API. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api
- [actionable] Respond to disputes based on their current lifecycle stages. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api
- [actionable] Automate dispute handling using webhooks. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api
- [fact] A PayPal developer account is required before using the Disputes API. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#prerequisites
- [concept] Accounts and environment must be set up according to the 'Set up accounts and environment' guide prior to API usage. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#prerequisites
- [concept] The inquiry stage is a pre-claim period where PayPal facilitates direct communication between parties to resolve disputes without intervention. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#understand-dispute-lifecycle-stages
- [fact] The inquiry stage applies only to internal disputes involving item/service not received (INR) or significantly not as described (SNAD). — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#understand-dispute-lifecycle-stages
- [fact] During the inquiry stage, the dispute_life_cycle_stage parameter value is INQUIRY. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#understand-dispute-lifecycle-stages
- [concept] The claim stage begins when a dispute is escalated to PayPal after failing resolution in the inquiry stage or for specific external disputes. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#understand-dispute-lifecycle-stages
- [fact] All external and non-merchandise disputes, such as incorrect charges or unauthorized transactions, proceed directly to the claim stage. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#understand-dispute-lifecycle-stages
- [concept] The CHARGEBACK stage authorizes PayPal to review and adjudicate a case where only agents can view buyer notes. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#understand-dispute-lifecycle-stages
- [fact] The CHARGEBACK lifecycle value is distinct from credit or debit card chargebacks. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#understand-dispute-lifecycle-stages
- [concept] PRE_ARBITRATION is the first appeal stage for merchants following an unfavorable chargeback outcome. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#understand-dispute-lifecycle-stages
- [concept] ARBITRATION is the second appeal stage available to merchants if the first appeal is denied. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#understand-dispute-lifecycle-stages
- [fact] If a merchant fails to appeal within the period, PayPal returns the case to resolved status in the PRE_ARBITRATION stage. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#understand-dispute-lifecycle-stages
- [fact] PayPal adjudicates internal dispute cases within 10 days of reviewing submitted evidence. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#understand-dispute-lifecycle-stages
- [fact] After resolution, the case status is updated to RESOLVED regardless of the lifecycle stage. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#understand-dispute-lifecycle-stages
- [problem] Merchants who do not wish to engage directly with buyers must opt out of the inquiry stage by contacting PayPal or their account manager. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#understand-dispute-lifecycle-stages
- [fact] The inquiry stage lasts up to 20 days unless escalated to a claim, after which an unresolved dispute is considered canceled. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#understand-dispute-lifecycle-stages
- [idea] Merchants eligible for Accelerated Response can submit supporting documentation within 10 days of the inquiry start. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#understand-dispute-lifecycle-stages
- [fact] Payroll notifies eligible merchants when the Accelerated Response enhancement becomes available on their accounts. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#understand-dispute-lifecycle-stages
- [concept] Webhook events are external events that your app does not know about unless it receives event notifications. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#use-webhooks-to-monitor-dispute-events
- [fact] Examples of webhook events include a new dispute being raised or resolved. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#use-webhooks-to-monitor-dispute-events
- [actionable] Review the list of webhook events for disputes and select the events for your app to subscribe. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#use-webhooks-to-monitor-dispute-events
- [actionable] Subscribe to selected webhook events through the PayPal developer account by logging in, going to App details > Features > Webhooks. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#use-webhooks-to-monitor-dispute-events
- [actionable] Subscribe to selected webhook events using the Webhooks management API. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#use-webhooks-to-monitor-dispute-events
- [concept] When a subscribed event occurs, PayPal sends a notification to the registered callback URL. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#use-webhooks-to-monitor-dispute-events
- [actionable] Define a webhook handler in your server-side app code that listens to the webhook event. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#use-webhooks-to-monitor-dispute-events
- [actionable] Process the webhook event message sent to your listener URL within your app code. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#use-webhooks-to-monitor-dispute-events
- [actionable] Verify the source of the event notification using the verify webhook signature functionality. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#use-webhooks-to-monitor-dispute-events
- [idea] Code your app to perform relevant actions based on the event notification it receives. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/handle-disputes/use-disputes-api#use-webhooks-to-monitor-dispute-events

## Overview
<https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/overview>

- [parameter] Buyer: Customer who makes a transaction for a product or service and initiates the dispute for that transaction. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/overview#parties-involved-in-dispute-management
- [parameter] Merchant: Seller who provides the product or service for the transaction. They monitor and respond to disputes. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/overview#parties-involved-in-dispute-management
- [parameter] PayPal: Payment processor that facilitates the transaction and helps you manage the dispute resolution process. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/overview#parties-involved-in-dispute-management
- [parameter] Bank or card issuer: Bank or financial institution that issued the buyer's payment instrument (for example, debit or credit card). They manage chargebacks and ACH returns. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/overview#parties-involved-in-dispute-management
- [parameter] Card network: Payment network that facilitates transactions and chargebacks between issuing and acquiring banks. It sets dispute resolution rules and timelines.<br />For example: Visa, Mastercard, American Express, and so on. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/overview#parties-involved-in-dispute-management
- [parameter] Partner: Platform that uses connected integration and onboards merchants through PayPal. In connected integration, merchants assume financial liability and handle disputes and chargebacks. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/overview#parties-involved-in-dispute-management
- [parameter] Item not received (INR): Buyer claims they ordered and paid for an item or service, but did not receive it. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/overview#common-buyer-issues
- [parameter] Significantly not as described (SNAD): Buyer received the item, but it does not match the merchant's description. It looks significantly different or has sustained damage during shipping. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/overview#common-buyer-issues
- [parameter] Billing or subscription errors: Buyer claims billing or subscription errors, such as duplicate charges, multiple transactions, or incorrect amounts. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/overview#common-buyer-issues
- [parameter] Unauthorized transactions: Buyer claims: <ul><li>They did not authorize the purchase, and the transaction occurred without their consent.</li><li>They were victims of debit or credit card fraud or identity theft.</li></ul> — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/overview#common-buyer-issues
- [parameter] Request for additional details: Buyer may require additional transaction details, such as a copy of the transaction or a receipt, to resolve uncertainties or discrepancies. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/overview#common-buyer-issues
- [parameter] Misdirected transactions: Buyer claims the payment went to an incorrect recipient or account. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/overview#common-buyer-issues
- [definition] Overview — A dispute or chargeback occurs when a buyer reports an issue with their transaction, such as non-receipt of an item, receiving an item significantly different from its description, discovering an unauthorized transaction, or identifying a billing error. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/overview#overview
- [definition] Understand disputes — You can use the information in this section to get familiar with PayPal's dispute management process. This helps you understand and manage disputes effectively. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/overview#understand-disputes
- [definition] Parties involved in dispute management — The following table outlines the key parties involved and their roles in the dispute management process: — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/overview#parties-involved-in-dispute-management
- [definition] Types of disputes — PayPal classifies disputes into [internal](#internal-disputes) and [external](#external-disputes) disputes based on where they originate and who manages adjudication. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/overview#types-of-disputes
- [definition] Dispute lifecycle and stages — Understanding the dispute lifecycle helps you manage disputes more efficiently. Disputes typically progress through these stages: — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/overview#dispute-lifecycle-and-stages
- [definition] Common buyer issues — The following table summarizes the common buyer issues that lead to disputes: — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/overview#common-buyer-issues
- [definition] How disputes work — This section provides an overview of the internal and external dispute workflows. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/overview#how-disputes-work
- [definition] Internal disputes — 1. **Buyer**: Files a dispute for a transaction with PayPal. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/overview#internal-disputes
- [definition] External disputes — 1. **Buyer**: Files a dispute for a transaction with the bank or card issuer. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/overview#external-disputes

## Dispute reasons and evidence
<https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons>

- [parameter] `PROOF_OF_FULFILLMENT`: Provide one of the following to PayPal:<ul><li>Carrier name and tracking number through `evidences.evidence_info.tracking_info.carrier_name` and `evidences.evidence_info.tracking_info.tracking_number` parameters respectively.</li>Or<li>Notes or documents in the `evidences.notes` or `evidences.documents` parameters respectively. For example, a document can be a proof of… — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#merchandise-or-service-not-received
- [parameter] `PROOF_OF_REFUND`: Pass the refund ID in the `evidences.evidence_info.refund_ids` parameter. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#merchandise-or-service-not-received
- [parameter] `OTHER`: Pass the notes or documents in the `evidences.notes` or `evidences.documents` parameters respectively. For example, you can include a product description, item URL, return policy, or a copy of a contract or an affidavit. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#merchandise-or-service-not-as-described
- [parameter] `PROOF_OF_REFUND`: Pass the refund ID in the `evidences.evidence_info.refund_ids` parameter. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#merchandise-or-service-not-as-described
- [parameter] `PROOF_OF_FULFILLMENT`: Provide the carrier name and tracking number through `evidences.evidence_info.tracking_info.carrier_name` and `evidences.evidence_info.tracking_info.tracking_number` parameters respectively. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#unauthorised
- [parameter] `PROOF_OF_REFUND`: Pass the refund ID in the `evidences.evidence_info.refund_ids` parameter. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#unauthorised
- [parameter] `OTHER`: Pass the notes or documents in the `evidences.notes` or `evidences.documents` parameters respectively. For example, a document can be a proof of delivery signature or a proof of receipt copy. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#unauthorised
- [parameter] `PROOF_OF_REFUND`: Pass the refund ID in the `evidences.evidence_info.refund_ids` parameter. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#credit-not-processed
- [parameter] `OTHER`: Pass the notes or documents in the `evidences.notes` or `evidences.documents` parameters respectively. For example, a document can be a credit due reason. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#credit-not-processed
- [parameter] `PROOF_OF_REFUND`: Pass the refund ID in the `evidences.evidence_info.refund_ids` parameter. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#duplicate-transaction
- [parameter] `OTHER`: Pass the notes or documents in the `evidences.notes` or `evidences.documents` parameters respectively. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#duplicate-transaction
- [parameter] `PROOF_OF_REFUND`: Pass the refund ID in the `evidences.evidence_info.refund_ids` parameter for the difference in amount. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#incorrect-amount
- [parameter] `OTHER`: Pass the notes or documents in the `evidences.notes` or `evidences.documents` parameters respectively. For example, a document can be a price difference reason. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#incorrect-amount
- [parameter] `PROOF_OF_REFUND`: Pass the refund ID in the `evidences.evidence_info.refund_ids` parameter for the difference in amount. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#payment-by-other-means
- [parameter] `OTHER`: Pass the notes or documents in the `evidences.notes` or `evidences.documents` parameters respectively. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#payment-by-other-means
- [parameter] `PROOF_OF_REFUND`: Pass the refund ID in the `evidences.evidence_info.refund_ids` parameter for the difference in amount. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#canceled-recurring-billing
- [parameter] `OTHER`: Pass the notes or documents in the `evidences.notes` or `evidences.documents` parameters respectively. For example, a document can be a subscription agreement. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#canceled-recurring-billing
- [parameter] `PROOF_OF_REFUND`: Pass the refund ID in the `evidences.evidence_info.refund_ids` parameter. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#other
- [parameter] `OTHER`: Pass the notes or documents in the `evidences.notes` or `evidences.documents` parameters respectively. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#other
- [definition] Dispute reasons and evidence — You can determine what evidence to provide for a dispute or chargeback based on its reason. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#dispute-reasons-and-evidence
- [definition] MERCHANDISE\_OR\_SERVICE\_NOT\_RECEIVED — The buyer did not receive the merchandise or service. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#merchandise-or-service-not-received
- [definition] MERCHANDISE\_OR\_SERVICE\_NOT\_AS\_DESCRIBED — The buyer reports that the merchandise or service was not as described. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#merchandise-or-service-not-as-described
- [definition] UNAUTHORISED — The buyer did not authorize purchase of the merchandise or service. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#unauthorised
- [definition] CREDIT\_NOT\_PROCESSED — The refund or credit transaction was not processed for the buyer. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#credit-not-processed
- [definition] INCORRECT\_AMOUNT — The buyer was charged an incorrect amount. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#incorrect-amount
- [definition] PAYMENT\_BY\_OTHER\_MEANS — The buyer paid for the transaction through other means. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#payment-by-other-means
- [definition] CANCELED\_RECURRING\_BILLING — The buyer was incorrectly charged after canceling the subscription or recurring billing. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/dispute-reasons#canceled-recurring-billing

- [concept] Test values are predefined inputs used to simulate negative Disputes API responses in a sandbox environment for development and testing. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/disputes-api-test-values
- [fact] Test values are case-sensitive. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/disputes-api-test-values

## Supported file types and sizes
<https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/supported-file-types-sizes>

- [definition] Supported file types and sizes — When handling disputes, you can upload supporting files as evidence to strengthen your case. Evidence can include receipts, delivery confirmations, photos, or other relevant documentation. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/supported-file-types-sizes#supported-file-types-and-sizes
- [fact] Supported file types for dispute evidence are .jpg, .jpeg, .gif, .png, and .pdf. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/supported-file-types-sizes
- [fact] The individual file size must be less than 10 MB per file. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/supported-file-types-sizes
- [fact] The total file size is limited to up to 50 MB per API call. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/supported-file-types-sizes
- [concept] Evidence for disputes can include receipts, delivery confirmations, photos, or other relevant documentation uploaded as supporting files. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/reference/supported-file-types-sizes

## Set up accounts and environment
<https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/set-up>

- [snippet] lines: <span id='cwppButton'></span> — `<span id='cwppButton'></span>` — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/set-up#set-up-sandbox-account
- [parameter] `DISPUTE_CREATE`: Scope=`https://uri.paypal.com/services/disputes/create`; Description=Scope to create a dispute. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/set-up#set-up-sandbox-account
- [parameter] `UPDATE_BUYER`: Scope=`https://uri.paypal.com/services/disputes/update-buyer`; Description=Scope to change the dispute reason. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/set-up#set-up-sandbox-account
- [definition] Set up accounts and environment — Use these procedures to configure your PayPal business and sandbox accounts to manage disputes in the Resolution Center or to integrate with the Disputes API. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/set-up#set-up-accounts-and-environment
- [concept] The sandbox environment is a test environment that mimics real-world transactions, automatically created upon signing up for a developer account. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/set-up
- [fact] By default, the sandbox environment includes one business and one personal account. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/set-up
- [actionable] To create disputes as a buyer in tests, use the personal sandbox account; to handle them, use the business sandbox account. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/set-up
- [concept] The Resolution Center path is for manually testing and managing disputes without integrating the Disputes API. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/set-up
- [actionable] To set up a sandbox app, go to Apps & Credentials, select Create App, enter an app name, and select Type as Merchant. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/set-up
- [fact] The Customer disputes checkbox is selected by default under Features > Add-on services for sandbox apps. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/set-up
- [actionable] To configure a webhook listener, go to Sandbox Webhooks, select Add Webhook, enter the Webhook URL, select events, and save. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/set-up
- [fact] The PayPal-Auth-Assertion header is required to create disputes or change dispute reasons as a buyer using the Disputes API. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/set-up
- [actionable] When generating a Log in with PayPal button, set the auth end point to sandbox and the scope to https://uri.paypal.com/services/disputes/create or https://uri.paypal.com/services/disputes/update-buyer. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/set-up
- [problem] The PayPal-Auth-Assertion header may be ignored or rejected if the JWT contains incorrect CLIENT_ID or BUYER_EMAIL values. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/set-up
- [actionable] To set up a live account, switch to the Live environment in the Developer Dashboard and go to Apps & Credentials. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/set-up
- [fact] For live apps, the Customer disputes checkbox is selected by default under Features > Other features. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/set-up
- [actionable] To download files submitted as evidence in production, contact your PayPal account manager to add the DOCUMENTS_DISPUTES_DOWNLOAD scope. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/set-up
- [fact] Partners must complete a specific form to set up a partner account with connected integration before configuring the Live account. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/set-up
- [actionable] To verify the source of webhook messages, store the Webhook ID displayed after configuration and use it in your app code. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/set-up

- [definition] Test webhooks — To test disputes webhooks, you need to wait a few minutes after creating the dispute for the actions to become available. You can then perform the actions to trigger the webhooks. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#test-webhooks

- [definition] Test dispute updated webhook — You can test the `CUSTOMER.DISPUTE.UPDATED` webhook in both the [inquiry](#test-dispute-updated-inquiry-stage) and [claim](#test-dispute-updated-claim-stage) stages. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#test-dispute-updated-webhook
- [definition] Test dispute resolved webhook — You can test the `CUSTOMER.DISPUTE.RESOLVED` webhook for these scenarios. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#test-dispute-resolved-webhook

- [fact] The target audience for this implementation process includes developers and QA engineers. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live
- [concept] Escalating a dispute to a claim simulates cases where an amicable resolution with the buyer cannot be reached. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#simulate-test-scenarios
- [fact] The `PROOF_OF_FULFILLMENT` evidence type is tested by providing tracking information for INR cases. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#simulate-test-scenarios
- [fact] The `PROOF_OF_REFUND` evidence type is tested by providing refund details for INR or SNAD cases. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#simulate-test-scenarios
- [fact] The `OTHER` evidence type allows providing notes or documents, including photos and files, for INR or SNAD cases. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#simulate-test-scenarios
- [problem] Merchants must have sufficient funds to issue a refund when accepting a claim. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#simulate-test-scenarios
- [fact] The test procedure for the `REFUND` accept claim type is identical in both inquiry and claim stages. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#simulate-test-scenarios
- [concept] The `REFUND_WITH_RETURN` option tests accepting a claim for a SNAD case and refunding the buyer after they return the item. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#simulate-test-scenarios
- [concept] The `PARTIAL_REFUND` option tests providing a refund amount less than the buyer-requested refund for INR or SNAD cases. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#simulate-test-scenarios
- [fact] The 'acknowledge returned item' option becomes available after offering a refund with return, accepting it as a buyer, and uploading return tracking information. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#simulate-test-scenarios
- [fact] PayPal uses predefined rules to determine appeal eligibility when adjudicating a case against a party. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#simulate-test-scenarios
- [actionable] To test seller or buyer appeal workflows, create a buyer account with a primary email address that includes `allowappeal` (e.g., testallowappeal@personal.example.com). — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#simulate-test-scenarios
- [fact] Disputes created with an `allowappeal` email trigger the seller appeal workflow if the seller is found liable and the buyer appeal workflow if the buyer is found liable. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#simulate-test-scenarios
- [problem] Each party can submit only one appeal per case. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#simulate-test-scenarios
- [actionable] To simulate cases where appeals are not allowed for a buyer, create a buyer account with a primary email address that includes `noappeal` (e.g., testnoappeal@personal.example.com). — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#simulate-test-scenarios
- [fact] The `PROOF_OF_FULFILLMENT` evidence type can be used to appeal if the case is resolved in the buyer's favor for INR, unauthorized, or other cases. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#simulate-test-scenarios
- [fact] The `PROOF_OF_REFUND` evidence type can be used to appeal if the case is resolved in the buyer's favor for all dispute reasons. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#simulate-test-scenarios
- [fact] The `OTHER` evidence type can be used to appeal if the requested evidence is not covered by `PROOF_OF_FULFILLMENT` or `PROOF_OF_REFUND`. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#simulate-test-scenarios
- [actionable] To enable Accelerated Response for a dispute, include the value ACCELERATED_RESPONSE_ENABLED in the notes field under evidences when submitting your create_dispute API request. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#accelerated-response-sandbox-testing
- [fact] Accelerated Response currently applies to dispute reasons MERCHANDISE_OR_SERVICE_NOT_AS_DESCRIBED and MERCHANDISE_OR_SERVICE_NOT_RECEIVED. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#accelerated-response-sandbox-testing
- [concept] To simulate the day of escalation in the sandbox environment, create a transaction with one of the listed amounts and use that transaction when creating the dispute. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#accelerated-response-sandbox-testing
- [actionable] Once the dispute is created, you can perform any actions listed under the Inquiry stage section. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#accelerated-response-sandbox-testing
- [concept] Applicable document proofs for disputes must adhere to specific documentation requirements. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#applicable-document-proofs
- [fact] The specific requirements for supported file types and sizes are defined in the Supported file types and sizes reference page. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#applicable-document-proofs
- [concept] To test disputes webhooks, you must wait a few minutes after creating a dispute for actions to become available. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#test-webhooks
- [fact] The CUSTOMER.DISPUTE.CREATED webhook can be tested in both the inquiry and claim stages. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#test-webhooks
- [actionable] Create a SNAD case for a transaction to test triggering the CUSTOMER.DISPUTE.CREATED webhook in the inquiry stage. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#test-webhooks
- [actionable] Create an INR case for a transaction to test triggering the CUSTOMER.DISPUTE.CREATED webhook in the inquiry stage. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#test-webhooks
- [fact] For SNAD and INR cases in the claim stage, you must have opted out of the dispute stage to test the webhook. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#test-webhooks
- [actionable] Create an unauthorized case for a transaction to test triggering the CUSTOMER.DISPUTE.CREATED webhook in the claim stage. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#test-webhooks
- [fact] For unauthorized disputes, the buyer and merchant receive different dispute IDs, though notification formats remain consistent. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#test-webhooks
- [actionable] Test triggering the webhook by creating a dispute for a transaction error using reasons like CREDIT_NOT_PROCESSED, DUPLICATE_TRANSACTION, INCORRECT_AMOUNT, PAYMENT_BY_OTHER_MEANS, CANCELED_RECURRING_BILLING, or OTHER. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#test-webhooks
- [problem] A buyer cannot open a duplicate dispute on a transaction that already has an existing dispute, even after the original case is closed. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#test-webhooks
- [fact] The CUSTOMER.DISPUTE.UPDATED webhook can be tested in both the inquiry and claim stages. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#test-webhooks
- [actionable] Test the CUSTOMER.DISPUTE.UPDATED webhook by sending a message to the merchant as a buyer for an INR or SNAD case in the inquiry stage. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#test-webhooks
- [actionable] To test the webhook in the claim stage, accept a claim, provide return shipping address, and have the buyer return the item with tracking information. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#test-webhooks
- [actionable] Test changing the dispute reason of an INR dispute to SNAD or unauthorized activity to trigger the CUSTOMER.DISPUTE.UPDATED webhook. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#test-webhooks
- [fact] The CUSTOMER.DISPUTE.RESOLVED webhook is triggered by specific resolution scenarios including buyer cancellation or settlement outcomes. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#test-webhooks
- [actionable] Test the CUSTOMER.DISPUTE.RESOLVED webhook by cancelling the dispute in the Resolution Center as a buyer. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#test-webhooks
- [actionable] Test the CUSTOMER.DISPUTE.RESOLVED webhook by settling the case in the buyer's favor or in the merchant's favor. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#test-webhooks
- [concept] You can test identifying a specific webhook by using the webhook ID found in the webhook payload. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#test-webhooks
- [actionable] To simulate Disputes API responses, inject test values as a path or query parameter in the request URL. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#test-webhooks
- [problem] A 400 Bad Request error occurs when required fields or formats are invalid during API testing. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#test-webhooks
- [problem] A 401 Unauthorized error occurs when the access token is missing, expired, or invalid. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#test-webhooks
- [problem] A 403 Forbidden error occurs when the app is not permitted to perform the action. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#test-webhooks
- [actionable] Set up a live account before going live. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#go-live
- [actionable] Set up webhooks to receive real-time notifications about dispute events such as created, updated, or resolved disputes. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#go-live
- [concept] Webhook messages include formats for dispute events like created, updated, or resolved disputes. — https://docs.paypal.ai/growth/disputes/test-go-live#go-live
